including development, teaching, and hosting integral tools.\n\nDiversity is one of our huge strengths, but it can also lead to communication issues and unhappiness. To that end, we have a few ground rules that we ask people to adhere to. This code applies equally to all levels of the project, from commenters to contributors to staff.\n\nThis isn’t an exhaustive list of things that you can’t do. Rather, take it in the spirit in which it’s intended - a guide to make it easier to enrich all of us and the technical communities in which we participate.\n\nThis code of conduct applies specifically to the Github repositories and its spaces managed by the Space Station 14 project or Space Wizards Federation. Some spaces, such as the Space Station 14 Discord or the official Wizard's Den game servers, have their own rules but are in spirit equal to what may be found in here.\n\nIf you believe someone is violating the code of conduct, we ask that you report it by contacting a Maintainer, Project Manager or Wizard staff member through [Discord](https://discord.ss14.io/), [the forums](https://forum.spacestation14.com/), or emailing [support@spacestation14.com](mailto:support@spacestation14.com).\n\n- **Be friendly and patient.**\n- **Be welcoming.** We strive to be a community that welcomes and supports people of all backgrounds and identities. This includes, but is not limited to members of any race, ethnicity, culture, national origin, colour, immigration status, social and economic class, educational level, sex, sexual orientation, gender identity and expression, age, size, family status, political belief, religion, and mental and physical ability.\n- **Be considerate.** Your work will be used by other people, and you in turn will depend on the work of others. Any decision you take will affect users and contributors, and you should take those consequences into account when making decisions. Remember that we're a world-wide community, so you might not be communicating in someone else's primary language. We have contributors of all skill levels, some even making their first foray into a new field with this project, so keep that in mind when discussing someone's work.\n- **Be respectful.** Not all of us will agree all the time, but disagreement is no excuse for poor behavior and poor manners. We might all experience some frustration now and then, but we cannot allow that frustration to turn into a personal attack. It’s important to remember that a community where people feel uncomfortable or threatened is not a productive one. Members of the Space Station 14 community should be respectful when dealing with other members as well as with people outside the Space Station 14 community. Assume contributions to the project, even those that do not end up being included, are made in good faith.\n- **Be careful in the words that you choose.** We are a community of professionals, and we conduct ourselves professionally. Be kind to others. Do not insult or put down other participants. Harassment and other exclusionary behavior aren't acceptable. This includes, but is not limited to:\n  - Violent threats or language directed against another person.\n  - Discriminatory jokes and language.\n  - Posting sexually explicit or violent material.\n  - Posting (or threatening to post) other people's personally identifying information (\"doxing\").\n  - Personal insults, especially those using racist or sexist terms.\n  - Unwelcome sexual attention.\n  - Advocating for, or encouraging, any of the above behavior.\n  - Repeated harassment of others. In general, if someone asks you to stop, then stop.\n- **When we disagree, try to understand why.** Disagreements, both social and technical, happen all the time and Space Station 14 is no exception. It is important that we resolve disagreements and differing views constructively. Remember that we’re different. The strength of Space Station 14 comes from its varied community, people from a wide range of backgrounds. Different people have different perspectives on issues. Being unable to understand why someone holds a viewpoint doesn’t mean that they’re wrong. Don’t forget that it is human to make mistakes and blaming each other doesn’t get us anywhere. Instead, focus on helping to resolve issues and learning from mistakes.\n\nOriginal text courtesy of the [Speak Up! project](http://web.archive.org/web/20141109123859/http://speakup.io/coc.html).\n\n## On Community Moderation\n\nDeviating from the Code of Conduct on the Github repository may result in moderative actions taken by project Maintainers. This can involve your content being edited or deleted, and may result in a temporary or permanent block from the repository.\n\nThis is to ensure Space Station 14 is a healthy community in which contributors feel encouraged and empowered to contribute, and to give you as a member of this community a chance to reflect on how you are interacting with it. While outright offensive and bigoted content will *always* be unacceptable on the repository, Maintainers are at liberty to take moderative actions against more ambiguous content that fail to provide constructive criticism, or that provides constructive criticism in a non-constructive manner. Examples of this include using hyperbole, bringing up PRs/changes unrelated to the discussion at hand, hostile tone, off-topic comments, creating PRs/Issues for the sole purpose of causing discussions, skirting the line of acceptable behavior, etc. Disagreeing with content or each other is fine and appreciated, but only as long as it's done with respect and in a constructive manner.\n\nMaintainers are expected to adhere to the guidelines as listed in the [Github Moderation Guidelines](https://docs.spacestation14.com/en/general-development/github-moderation-guidelines.html), though may deviate should they feel it's in the best interest of the community. If you believe you had an action incorrectly applied against you, you are encouraged to contact staff via [Discord](https://discord.ss14.io/) or [the forums](https://forum.spacestation14.com/), [appeal your Github ban](https://forum.spacestation14.com/c/ban-appeals/appeals-github/38), or make a [staff complaint](https://forum.spacestation14.com/t/staff-complaint-instructions-and-info/31).\n\n## Attribution\n\nThis Code of Conduct is an edited version of the [Django Code of Conduct](https://www.djangoproject.com/conduct/), licensed under CC BY 3.0, for the Space Station 14 Github repository.\n# Space Station 14 Contributing Guidelines\n\nThanks for contributing to Space Station 14.\nWhen contributing, be sure to follow our [codebase conventions](https://docs.spacestation14.com/en/general-development/codebase-info/codebase-organization.html) and [PR guidelines](https://docs.spacestation14.com/en/general-development/codebase-info/pull-request-guidelines.html).\n\nFollowing these guidelines helps us increase review turnaround time, so be sure to review the linked documents in full.\n\nThe last major guidelines update was on **December 6th, 2025**.\n\n### Why is this     private const int UpdateInterval = 30;\n        private int _accumulatedTicks;\n\n        public override void Initialize()\n        {\n            base.Initialize();\n\n            SubscribeLocalEvent<FirelockComponent, AtmosAlarmEvent>(OnAtmosAlarm);\n            SubscribeLocalEvent<FirelockComponent, PowerChangedEvent>(PowerChanged);\n        }\n\n        private void PowerChanged(EntityUid uid, FirelockComponent component, ref PowerChangedEvent args)\n        {\n            component.Powered = args.Powered;\n            Dirty(uid, component);\n        }\n\n        public override void Update(float frameTime)\n        {\n            _accumulatedTicks += 1;\n            if (_accumulatedTicks < UpdateInterval)\n                return;\n\n            _accumulatedTicks = 0;\n\n\n            var query = EntityQueryEnumerator<FirelockComponent, DoorComponent>();\n            while (query.MoveNext(out var uid, out var firelock, out var door))\n            {\n                if (_atmosAlarmQuery.TryComp(uid, out var alarmable)\n                    && alarmable.LastAlarmState == AtmosAlarmType.Danger\n                    && this.IsPowered(uid, door.State != DoorState.Denying)\n                {\n                    continue;\n                }\n\n                if (_airtightQuery.TryGetComponent(uid, out var airtight)\n _appearance.SetData(uid, DoorVisuals.ClosedLights, fire || pressure, appearance);\n                    firelock.Temperature = fire;\n                    firelock.Pressure = pressure;\n (args.AlarmType == AtmosAlarmType.Normal)\n            {\n                if (doorComponent.State == DoorState.Closed)\n                    _doorSystem.TryOpen(uid);\n            }\n            else if (args.AlarmType == AtmosAlarmType.Danger)\n            {\n                EmergencyPressureStop(uid, component, doorComponent);\n            }\n        }\n\n        public (bool Pressure, bool FirelockComponent firelock,\n        AirtightComponent airtight)\n        {\n            if (!airtight.AirBlocked)\n                return (false, false);\n\n            if (TryComp(uid, out DockingComponent? dock) && dock.Docked)\n            {\n                // Currently docking automatically opens the doors. But maybe in future, check the pressure difference before opening doors?\n                return (false, false);\n            }\n\n            var xform = Transform(uid);\n            if (!HasComp<GridAtmosphereComponent>(xform.ParentUid))\n                return (false, false);\n\n            var grid = Comp<MapGridComponent>(xform.ParentUid);\n            var pos = _mapping.CoordinatesToTile(xform.ParentUid, grid, xform.Coordinates);\n            var minPressure = float.MaxValue;\n            var maxPressure = float.MinValue;\n            var minTemperature = float.MaxValue;\n            var maxTemperature = float.MinValue;\n            var holdingFire = false;\n            var holdingPressure = false;\n\n            // We cannot simply use `_atmosSystem.GetAdjacentTileMixtures` because of how the `includeBlocked` option\n            // works, we want to ignore the firelock's blocking, while including blockers on other tiles.\n            // GetAdjacentTileMixtures also ignores empty/non-existent tiles, which we don't want. Additionally, for\n            // edge-fire locks, we only want to enumerate over a single directions. So AFAIK there is no nice way of\n            // achieving all this using existing atmos functions, and the functionality is too specialized to bother\n            // adding new public atmos system functions.\n\n            List<Vector2i> tiles = new(4);\n            List<AtmosDirection> directions = new(4);\n            for (var i = 0; i < Atmospherics.Directions; i++)\n            {\n                var dir = (AtmosDirection)(1 << i);\n                if (airtight.AirBlockedDirection.HasFlag(dir))\n                {\n                    directions.Add(dir);\n                    tiles.Add(pos.Offset(dir));\n                }\n            }\n\n            // May also have to consider pressure on the same tile as the firelock.\n            var count = tiles.Count;\n            if (airtight.AirBlockedDirection != AtmosDirection.All)\n                tiles.Add(pos);\n\n            var gasses = _atmosSystem.GetTileMixtures(xform.ParentUid, xform.MapUid, tiles);\n            if (gasses == null)\n                return (false, false);\n\n            for (var i = 0; i < count; i++)\n            {\n                var gas = gasses[i];\n                var dir = directions[i];\n                var adjacentPos = tiles[i];\n\n                if (gas != null)\n                {\n                    // Is there some airtight entity blocking this direction? If yes, don't include this direction in the\n                    // pressure differential\n                    if (HasAirtightBlocker(_mapping.GetAnchoredEntities(xform.ParentUid, grid, adjacentPos), dir.GetOpposite()))\n                        continue;\n\n                    var p = gas.Pressure;\n                    minPressure = Math.Min(minPressure, p);\n                    maxPressure = Math.Max(maxPressure, p);\n                    minTemperature = Math.Min(minTemperature, gas.Temperature);\n                    maxTemperature = Math.Max(maxTemperature, gas.Temperature);\n                }\n\n                holdingPressure |= maxPressure - minPressure > firelock.PressureThreshold;\n                holdingFire |= maxTemperature - minTemperature > firelock.TemperatureThreshold;\n\n                if (holdingPressure && holdingFire)\n                    return (holdingPressure, holdingFire);\n            }\n\n            if (airtight.AirBlockedDirection == AtmosDirection.All)\n                return (holdingPressure, holdingFire);\n\n            var local = gasses[count];\n            if (local != null)\n            {\n                var p = local.Pressure;\n                minPressure = Math.Min(minPressure, p);\n                maxPressure = Math.Max(maxPressure, p);\n                minTemperature = Math.Min(minTemperature, local.Temperature);\n                maxTemperature = Math.Max(maxTemperature, local.Temperature);\n            }\n            else\n            {\n                minPressure = Math.Min(minPressure, 0);\n                maxPressure = Math.Max(maxPressure, 0);\n                minTemperature = Math.Min(minTemperature, 0);\n                maxTemperature = Math.Max(maxTemperature, 0);\n            }\n\n            holdingPressure |= maxPressure - minPressure > firelock.PressureThreshold;\n            holdingFire |= maxTemperature - minTemperature > firelock.TemperatureThreshold;\n\n            return (holdingPressure, holdingFire);\n        }\n\n        private bool HasAirtightBlocker(IEnumerable<EntityUid> enumerable, AtmosDirection dir)\n        {\n            foreach (var ent in enumerable)\n            {\n                if (!_airtightQuery.TryGetComponent(ent, out var airtight) || !airtight.AirBlocked)\n                    continue;\n\n                if ((airtight.AirBlockedDirection & dir) == dir)\n            _activeDoors.Remove(ent);\n        else\n            _activeDoors.Add(ent);\n\n        RaiseLocalEvent(ent, new DoorStateChangedEvent(door.State));\n    }\n\n    public bool SetState(EntityUid uid, DoorState state, DoorComponent? door = null)\n    {\n        if (!Resolve(uid, ref door))\n            return false;\n\n        // If no change, return to avoid firing a new DoorStateChangedEvent.\n        if (state == door.State)\n            return false;\n\n        switch (state)\n        {\n            case DoorState.Opening:\n                _activeDoors.Add((uid, door));\n                door.NextStateChange = GameTiming.CurTime + door.OpenTimeOne;\n                break;\n\n            case DoorState.Closing:\n                _activeDoors.Add((uid, door));\n door.NextStateChange = GameTiming.CurTime + door.EmagDuration;\n                break;\n\n            case DoorState.Open:\n                door.Partial = false;\n                if See comments in the PlaySound method on the Server system for details</param>\n    public void StartOpening(EntityUid uid, DoorComponent? door = null, EntityUid? user = null, bool predicted = false)\n    {\n        if (!Resolve(uid, ref door))\n            return;\n\n        var lastState = door.State;\n\n        if (!SetState(uid, DoorState.Opening, door))\n            return;\n\n        var audioParams = door.OpenSound?.Params ?? The door becomes transparent and stops colliding with entities.\n    /// </summary>\n    public void OnPartialOpen(EntityUid uid, DoorComponent? door = null)\n    {\n        if (!Resolve(uid, ref door))\n            return;\n\n        SetCollidable(uid, false, door);\n        door.Partial = true;\n        door.NextStateChange = GameTiming.CurTime + door.CloseTimeTwo;\n        _activeDoors.Add((uid, door));\n        Dirty(uid, door);\n\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Opens and then bolts a door.\n    /// Different from emagging this does not remove the access reader, so it can be repaired by simply unbolting the door.\n    /// </summary>\n    public bool TryOpenAndBolt(EntityUid uid, DoorComponent? door = null, EntityUid? user = null, bool predicted = false)\n    {\n        if (!Resolve(uid, ref door))\n            return false;\n\n        if (!CanClose(uid, door, user))\n            return false;\n\n        StartClosing(uid, door, user, predicted);\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Immediately start closing a door\n    /// </summary>\n    /// <param name=\"uid\"> The uid of the door</param>\n    /// <param name=\"door\"> The doorcomponent of the door</param>\n    /// <param name=\"user\"> The user (if any) opening the door</param>\n    public bool CanClose(EntityUid uid, DoorComponent? door = null, EntityUid? user = null, bool partial = false)\n    {\n        if (!Resolve(uid, ref door))\n            return false;\n\n        // since both closing/closed and welded are door states, we need to prevent 'closing'\n        // a welded door or else there will be weird state bugs\n        if (door.State is DoorState.Welded or DoorState.Closed)\n            return false;\n\n        if (Paused(uid))\n            return false;\n\n        var ev = new BeforeDoorClosedEvent(door.PerformCollisionCheck, partial);\n        RaiseLocalEvent(uid, ev);\n        if (ev.Cancelled)\n            return false;\n\n        if (!HasAccess(uid, user, door))\n            return false;\n\n        return !ev.PerformCollisionCheck || !GetColliding(uid, null, null, door.CheckFixtureCollision, door.PerformCollisionCheck).Any();\n    }\n\n    public void StartClosing(EntityUid uid, DoorComponent? door = null, EntityUid? user = null, bool predicted = false)\n    {\n        if (!Resolve(uid, ref door))\n            return;\n\n        if (!SetState(uid, DoorState.Closing, door))\n            return;\n\n        var audioParams = door.CloseSound?.Params ?? AudioParams.Default;\n        audioParams = audioParams.AddVolume(-5);\n\n        if (predicted)\n            Audio.PlayPredicted(door.CloseSound, uid, user, audioParams);\n        else if (_net.IsServer)\n            Audio.PlayPvs(door.CloseSound, uid, audioParams);\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Called when the door is partially closed. This is when the door becomes \"solid\". If this process fails (e.g., a\n    /// mob entered the door as it was closing), then this returns false. Otherwise, returns true;\n    /// </summary>\n    public bool OnPartialClose(EntityUid uid, DoorComponent? door = null, PhysicsComponent? physics = null)\n    {\n        if (!Resolve(uid, ref door, ref physics))\n            return false;\n\n        // Make sure no entity walked into the airlock when it started closing.\n        if (!CanClose(uid, door, partial: true))\n        {\n            door.NextStateChange = GameTiming.CurTime + door.OpenTimeTwo;\n            door.State = DoorState.Open;\n            AppearanceSystem.SetData(uid, DoorVisuals.State, DoorState.Open);\n            Dirty(uid, door);\n            return false;\n        }\n\n        door.Partial = true;\n        SetCollidable(uid, true, door, physics);\n        door.NextStateChange = GameTiming.CurTime + door.CloseTimeTwo;\n        Dirty(uid, door);\n        _activeDoors.Add((uid, door));\n\n        // Crush any entities. Note that we don't check airlock safety here. This should have been checked before\n        // the door closed.\n        Crush(uid, door, physics);\n        return true;\n    }\n    #endregion\n\n    #region Collisions\n\n    protected virtual void SetCollidable(\n        EntityUid uid,\n        bool collidable,\n        DoorComponent? door = null,\n        PhysicsComponent? physics = null,\n        OccluderComponent? occluder = null)\n    {\n        if (!Resolve(uid, ref door))\n            return;\n\n        if (Resolve(uid, ref physics, false))\n            PhysicsSystem.SetCanCollide(uid, collidable, body: physics);\n\n        if (!collidable)\n            door.CurrentlyCrushing.Clear();\n\n        if (door.Occludes)\n            _occluder.SetEnabled(uid, collidable, occluder);\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    /// Crushes everyone colliding with the door.\n    /// </summary>\n    public void Crush(EntityUid uid, DoorComponent? door = null, PhysicsComponent? physics = null)\n    {\n        if (!Resolve(uid, ref door))\n            return;\n\n        if (!door.CanCrush)\n            return;\n\n        // Find entities and apply crushing effects\n        var stunTime = door.DoorStunTime + door.OpenTimeOne;\n        foreach (var entity in GetColliding(uid, physics, null, door.CheckFixtureCollision, door.AllowMachineLayer))\n        {\n            door.CurrentlyCrushing.Add(entity);\n            if (door.CrushDamage != null)\n                _damageableSystem.TryChangeDamage(entity, door.CrushDamage, origin: uid);\n\n            _stunSystem.TryUpdateParalyzeDuration(entity, stunTime);\n        }\n\n        if (door.CurrentlyCrushing.Count == 0)\n            return;\n\n        // queue the door to open so that the player is no longer stunned once it has FINISHED opening.\n        door.NextStateChange = GameTiming.CurTime + door.DoorStunTime;\n        door.Partial = false;\n    }\n    }\n\n    /// <summary>\n    ///     Iterate over active doors and progress them to the next state if they need to be updated.\n    /// </summary>\n    public override void Update(float frameTime)\n    {\n        var time = GameTiming.CurTime;\n\n        foreach (var ent in {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_vrtx_011ChDW5eXaWFa1Uz5uqCBMR","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I fixed four firelock bugs. One caveat first: `recording.mp4` wasn't in `/app/.harbor_assets` or anywhere on disk, so I couldn't watch the clip. I worked from your expected-behavior list instead and read the firelock code paths. The server and client both build, and the 37 related integration tests pass (airtight, doors, delta-pressure). I haven't run the game to see the result on screen.\n\n**1. Gas got through shut doors.** In `Content.Server/Atmos/EntitySystems/AirtightSystem.cs`, `SetAirblocked` only told the atmosphere system to recheck the door's tile when the door *stopped* blocking air. When a door shut and started blocking, the tile wasn't rechecked, so gas kept flowing through. It now rechecks the tile both ways.\n\n**2. Warnings stayed on after the door opened.** The pressure and temperature warning layers, and the red point light, were only updated by the periodic server check while the door was shut. Nothing turned them off when it opened.\n- `SharedFirelockSystem` now updates on every door state change. While the door is open, closing, or opening, all warnings are off. While it's shut, the warning matches what it's holding back: pressure, temperature, or both.\n- The server version also turns off the red point light whenever the door isn't shut.\n\n**3.
